o Non-aggressive; doesn’t fight back. 
• Movement Speed: 4 blocks/second (burst speed); 1 block/second (normal 
hopping)  
o Fast when fleeing, slow when idle. 
• Armor: 0  
o No natural protection. 
• Spawn Rate: Common (e.g., 5-10 per chunk in suitable biomes). 
• Drops:  
o Rabbit Hide (50% chance, 1 unit). 
o Raw Rabbit Meat (75% chance, 1 unit). 
o Rabbit Foot (5% chance, rare crafting item).

Strengths 
1. Burst Speed:  
a. When startled, it leaps at high speed (4 blocks/second for 3-5 seconds), 
making it tricky to catch without a trap or ranged weapon. 
b. Game Effect: +50% evasion chance when fleeing. 
2. Keen Awareness:  
a. Its sharp senses detect threats within 10 blocks, triggering an immediate 
escape response before most predators can close in. 
b. Game Effect: Immune to surprise attacks from melee range. 
3. Camouflage:  
a. In tall grass or matching terrain (e.g., brown fur in plains), it blends in slightly, 
reducing visibility (e.g., +1 stealth vs. player detection). 
b. Game Effect: 20% chance to avoid detection unless within 3 blocks. 
4. Rapid Reproduction:  
a. In safe conditions, Plains Rabbits spawn frequently, ensuring their 
population recovers quickly from losses. 
b. Game Effect: Spawns in pairs or small groups (2-4) in ideal biomes. 
5. Small Target:  
a. Its tiny size makes it harder to hit with projectiles or sweeping attacks (e.g., -
1 to hit chance for ranged attacks beyond 5 blocks). 
 
Weaknesses 
1. Low Health:  
a. With only 4 HP, a single strike from any weapon (e.g., 5 damage from a 
wooden sword) or predator kills it instantly. 
b. Game Effect: No resistance to damage. 
2. Defenseless:  
a. Lacking any attack, it relies entirely on flight, posing no threat even if 
cornered. 
b. Game Effect: Damage output = 0. 
3. Predator Magnet:

4. Terrain Dependence:

second stun), leaving it open to attack or capture. 
b. Game Effect: Stunned by sound-based effects within 8 blocks. 
 
Behavior and Lore 
Plains Rabbits hop idly across fields, nibbling vegetation and digging small burrows to rest. 
They’re timid, fleeing from players, mobs, or sudden movements, often in zigzag patterns 
to confuse pursuers. Dwarves see them as a minor resource, trading their hides for Kupdun 
coins or roasting their meat over campfires. In large numbers, they can strip a field bare, 
earning the ire of farmers. 
In gameplay, they’re a basic survival target—easy to hunt for food or materials but 
requiring quick reflexes or traps (e.g., fences, leads) to catch. Their presence adds life to a 
biome, though their predator-drawing nature might surprise the unwary.

within 3 blocks), adding unpredictability. 
b. Game Effect: Rare “steal” mechanic.

Behavior and Lore 
• Speckled Finch: Cheerful and flighty, they brighten fields and towns, but their noise 
invites trouble. Farmers tolerate them for pest control. 
• Crested Sparrow: Bold and adaptable, they’re a common sight in bustling areas, 
dodging boots and snagging crumbs. 
• Dusky Crow: Clever and ominous, they circle battlefields or roost near taverns, 
eyed warily by dwarves who blame them for missing Kupdun coins. 
 
6.Name: Bristleback Hog 
Description 
The Bristleback Hog is a burly, aggressive beast, standing 3 feet at the shoulder and 
stretching 5 feet long, with a thick, bristly coat of dark brown or black hair streaked with 
gray. Coarse, spiky tufts run along its spine, giving it a jagged silhouette, while its small, 
bloodshot eyes glare beneath a pair of curved, yellowed tusks. Its muscular frame ripples 
as it snorts and paws the ground, and its hooves leave deep gouges in dirt or mud. A 
rancid, musky odor wafts from its hide, marking its territory in forests, plains, or swampy 
hollows. 
Bristleback Hogs roam wildlands and occasionally wander near towns, foraging for roots, 
berries, or carrion. They’re territorial and quick to charge when provoked, making them a 
risky encounter for the unprepared. 
 
Video Game Statistics 
• Health Points (HP): 20

o Decent durability, requiring multiple hits to bring down.

o Tough hide offers slight protection.

o Tusk Fragment (10% chance, rare item).

2. Tough Hide:  
a. Its bristly skin grants 2 armor points, reducing damage slightly (e.g., -2 
damage from attacks), making it resilient to weak blows. 
b. Game Effect: Absorbs minor hits (e.g., 1-2 damage negated). 
3. Territorial Fury:  
a. Gains a boost near its spawn point or herd (e.g., +1 damage and +5 HP if 
within 10 blocks of home biome), defending its turf fiercely. 
b. Game Effect: Enhanced stats in familiar territory. 
4. Foraging Stamina:  
a. Can sustain itself on varied diets, thriving in diverse biomes without tiring. 
b. Game Effect: Immune to hunger or exhaustion debuffs. 
5. Herd Instinct:  
a. In rare groups (2-3), they coordinate, charging in sequence to overwhelm 
threats. 
b. Game Effect: +1 attack speed when with allies. 
 

--- PAGE 248 ---
Weaknesses 
1. Limited Agility:  
a. Slow to turn during a charge, leaving it open after missing (e.g., -2 evasion for

b. Game Effect: Vulnerable to flanking attacks. 
2. Fire Susceptibility:  
a. Its thick hair catches fire easily, taking extra damage (e.g., +3 fire damage)

b. Game Effect: Weak to flame-based attacks. 
3. Noise Aggro:

recklessly toward the source, even into traps. 
b. Game Effect: 50% chance to aggro on sound within 15 blocks.

b. Game Effect: -2 detection range for silent targets. 
5. Overconfidence:  
a. Won’t flee even when badly hurt, fighting to the death once engaged. 
b. Game Effect: No retreat mechanic, predictable in combat. 
 
Behavior and Lore 
Bristleback Hogs root through underbrush or mud, grunting and snuffling as they search for 
food. They’re neutral unless approached too closely (e.g., within 5 blocks) or attacked, at 
which point they lower their heads and charge with a guttural squeal. Dwarves prize their 
meat and hides, trading Kupdun coins for them, but warn of their temper near mines or 
camps. In herds, they’re a rare but formidable sight, trampling anything in their path. 
In gameplay, they’re a step up from rabbits or birds—huntable for hearty resources but 
dangerous if mishandled. A torch or well-timed dodge can turn their charge into an 
advantage, leaving them open to counterattacks.

7.Name: Swiftantler Deer 
Description 
The Swiftantler Deer is a graceful, slender creature, standing 4 feet at the shoulder, with a 
lithe body cloaked in a soft, tawny coat speckled with faint white spots along its flanks. Its 
large, liquid-brown eyes gleam with alertness, framed by a pair of branching antlers—
sharp and sturdy in males, smaller and sleeker in females. Its long legs end in delicate 
hooves that barely touch the ground as it bounds through forests or plains, and a short, 
flicking tail signals its mood. A faint, earthy scent trails it, blending with the rustle of leaves 
or grass. 
Swiftantler Deer roam woodlands, meadows, and the edges of wild biomes, grazing on 
tender shoots or nibbling bark. They’re skittish, bolting at the slightest threat, but their 
elegance makes them a prized sight—and target—for hunters.

b. Game Effect: -50% sprint speed on rough terrain. 
 
Behavior and Lore 
Swiftantler Deer graze peacefully in open areas, lifting their heads to scan for danger 
between bites. They’re neutral unless approached too closely (e.g., within 6 blocks) or 
attacked, at which point they bound away in arcing leaps. Dwarves hunt them for their 
tender meat and hides, trading Zirdun coins for antlers to carve into tools or trophies. In 
herds, their synchronized flight is a mesmerizing sight—until a predator scatters them. 
In gameplay, they’re a rewarding hunt—rich in resources but elusive without ranged 
weapons, traps, or stealth. A bow or a well-placed fence can secure their loot, but their 
speed keeps them just out of reach for the clumsy.

Moderate-Level Monsters (Levels 10–15) in Valtherion Online 
Overview 
Across Valtherion’s mid-tier wilds—from the fiery wastes of Emberfield to the shadowy 
depths of Shadowfen—these moderate-level monsters challenge players with sub-
classes at Level 10. Warped by Zorvathax’s influence or shaped by the realm’s ancient 
forces, they guard valuable resources, offering Kupdun, Zirdun, crafting materials, and 
rare Skill Books. Below are the five original monsters, joined by a new Kobold (Cavern-
Scurry Variant) replacing the prior Kobold entry. 
 
1. Emberfang Salamander (Monster - Fire Reptilian) 
Description 
The Emberfang Salamander is a sinuous, 3-meter-long lizard with scales that shimmer 
like molten lava, glowing a deep orange-red along its sleek, muscular frame. Its ember-
bright eyes glare with predatory intent, and its wide maw drips with searing venom that 
hisses on contact with stone. A ridge of jagged, heat-warped spines runs down its back, 
pulsing with fiery energy. Found in Kaltheron’s lava fields or Emberfield’s volcanic 
outskirts, this once-benign creature now guards ore deposits, its fiery presence a 
testament to Zorvathax’s corrupting touch. 

--- PAGE 258 ---
Stats 
• Level: 12 
• HP: 150 
• Attack: 10–15 (fire-based) 
• Defense: 5 armor 
• Speed: 2 blocks/second 
• Special: Flame Burst (12–18 fire AoE, 3 blocks, 15-sec cooldown); Scorching Bite 
(15–20 fire + 3 DoT, 3 sec, 10-sec cooldown) 
Lore 
Once peaceful denizens of Kaltheron’s geothermal pools, these salamanders were 
twisted by Zorvathax, now serving as fiery sentinels of mineral wealth in Emberfield. 
Strengths 
• Fire Resistance: +50% reduction to fire damage. 
• High Burst Damage: Devastates in close range. 
• Heat Aura: 10% chance to deal 2 fire damage/sec within 2 blocks. 
Weaknesses 
• Ice Vulnerability: +50% ice damage taken. 
• Slow Movement: Easily kited by ranged classes. 
• Cooling Fatigue: -1 speed after using Flame Burst. 
Loot 
• Kupdun: 100–150 (2–3 Zirdun equivalent) 
• Zirdun: 1–2 (60% chance) 
• Ember Scales: 60% chance (5–10, Blacksmith/Armorer fire gear) 
• Chest: 10% chance - Salamander Hoard (50–100 Kupdun, 1 Lava Rock) 
• Scroll: 5% chance - Scroll of Ember Shield (+3 fire resist, 1 min) 
• Weapon/Equipment: 20% chance - Emberfang Claw (Uncommon, 10 durability, 
8–12 fire damage) 

--- PAGE 259 ---
• Item: 30% chance - Salamander Hide (1–3, Leatherworker fire armor); 10% chance 
- Skill Book: Flame Burst (Elementalist) or Tempered Strike (Blacksmith) 
Behavior 
Guarding ore with fiery bursts, they’re vulnerable to ice or kiting, yielding heat-forged 
spoils.

Lore 
Empowered by Zorvathax’s runes, these marauders plunder Valtherion’s edges, their loot 
a dark bounty for crafters seeking runic secrets. 
Strengths 
• High HP: Durable for mid-tier fights. 
• Physical Resistance: +20% reduction to physical. 
• Rune Chaos: Random effects disrupt foes. 
Weaknesses 
• Magic Vulnerability: +30% magic damage taken. 
• Predictable Patterns: Countered by mobility. 
• Rune Overload: -1 armor after Rune Burst. 
Loot 
• Kupdun: 175–250 (3.5–5 Zirdun equivalent) 
• Zirdun: 3–5 (60% chance) 
• Stolen Scrap: 60% chance (5–10, Tinker/Blacksmith) 
• Chest: 10% chance - Marauder’s Loot (80–150 Kupdun, 1 Rune Dust) 
• Scroll: 5% chance - Scroll of Rune Strike (12–16 damage, random effect) 

--- PAGE 265 ---
• Weapon/Equipment: 20% chance - Rune Blade (Uncommon, 10 durability, 10–14 
damage) 
• Item: 30% chance - Rune Shard (1–3, Enchanter/Runesmith); 10% chance - Skill 
Book: Trap Tuning (Tinker) or Holy Inscription (Runesmith) 
Behavior 
Raiding with slashes and runes, they’re felled by magic or evasion, yielding runic treasures. 
 
6. Kobold (Cavern-Scurry Variant) (Monster - Trap Kobold) 
Description 
The Kobold (Cavern-Scurry Variant) is a wiry, 3-foot-tall creature, its hunched frame 
cloaked in a scaly hide that shifts between mottled browns, grays, and dull greens, 
blending seamlessly into Ironwick’s caverns or Drakemire’s mines. Its large, amber eyes 
gleam with cunning, and its twitching snout, lined with jagged teeth, sniffs the air for 
threats or treasure. Thin, clawed hands grip a crude sling or stolen pick, while a whip-like 
tail aids its balance in tight tunnels. Small, horn-like ridges crown its head, a faint echo of 
draconic lineage, and a satchel of scavenged trinkets—bits of ore, broken tools—jingles at 
its side. 
Stats 
• Level: 15 
• HP: 90 (triple small Kobolds’ ~30 HP max) 
• Attack: 9–15 (physical) (triple small Kobolds’ 2–4) 
• Defense: 4 armor (double small Kobolds’ 2) 
• Speed: 4 blocks/second (exceeds small Kobolds’ 2–3) 
• Special: Trap Spike (12–18 damage, 3-block trap AoE, 10% slow, 12-sec cooldown); 
Skitter Swarm (summons 1 small Kobold [HP 20, Attack 2–4], 20-sec cooldown) 
Comparison to Small Kobolds 
• Small Kobolds (base): Level 1–5, HP 20–30, Attack 2–4, Armor 1–2, Speed 2–3 
blocks/second. 

--- PAGE 266 ---
• Cavern-Scurry Variant: HP tripled (90 vs. 30), Attack tripled (9–15 vs. 2–4), Armor 
doubled (4 vs. 2), Speed increased (4 vs. 2–3) for agility. 
Lore 
Evolved from the skittish Tunnel-Skitter Kobolds of Valtherion’s depths, this variant thrives 
in Ironwick’s mines or Drakemire’s warrens, leading lesser kin with trap-laden cunning. 
Obsessed with hoarding, they clash with Auric Slimes over minerals, revering a mythical 
“Great Wyrm” with crude shrines. 
Strengths 
• Trap Mastery: Triggers traps (2–4 damage) in a 5-block radius. 
• Agility: +2 evasion, excels in tunnels. 
• Pack Tactics: +1 damage per nearby Kobold (max +3). 
Weaknesses 
• Light Vulnerability: +50% light damage taken. 
• Fragile Frame: Low HP for Level 15, weak to bursts. 
• Greed Distraction: 50% chance to pause for shiny bait. 
Loot 
• Kupdun: 175–225 (3.5–4.5 Zirdun equivalent) 
• Zirdun: 3–5 (60% chance) 
• Kobold Meat: 60% chance (5–10, taming wild monsters) 
• Chest: 10% chance - Tunnel Hoard (80–140 Kupdun, 1 Ore Bit) 
• Scroll: 5% chance - Scroll of Trap Spike (10–14 damage, 2-block AoE) 
• Weapon/Equipment: 60% chance - Random (1 of 5):  
o Used Rusty Dagger (Uncommon, 10 durability, 5–8 damage) 
o Used Rusty Shortsword (Uncommon, 10 durability, 6–10 damage) 
o Used Chipped Arrow (Uncommon, 5 uses, 4–6 damage) 
o Used Worn Bow (Uncommon, 10 durability, 5–9 damage) 
o Used 3-Item Slot Bag (Uncommon, +3 inventory slots) 
• Item: 30% chance - Kobold Tail (1–3, Tinker traps); 10% chance - Skill Book: Trap 
Tuning (Tinker) or Keen Fletching (Fletcher) 

--- PAGE 267 ---
Behavior 
Skittering with traps and slings, they summon allies, undone by light or brute force, offering 
scavenged loot. 
 
Loot System Details 
• Chests: 10% chance, themed (e.g., Salamander Hoard, Tunnel Hoard), with 
Kupdun/Zirdun and flavor items. 
• Scrolls: 5% chance, combat boosts (e.g., Scroll of Ember Shield, Scroll of Trap 
Spike). 
• Weapons/Equipment: 20% (monsters) or 60% (Kobold), Uncommon-tier (e.g., 
Emberfang Claw, Used Worn Bow). 
• Items: 30% uncommon (e.g., Salamander Hide, Kobold Tail), 10% rare Skill Books 
(e.g., Venom Blade). 
• Kupdun/Zirdun: 100–250 Kupdun, 1–5 Zirdun, scaling with level, no Guldun. 
 
Integration with Valtherion 
• Regions: Salamanders in Emberfield, Creepers in Eldergrove, Boars in 
Wheatshadow, Arachnids in Shadowfen, Marauders in Stonegale, Kobolds in 
Ironwick. 
• Monetary Tie: Kupdun/Zirdun fund crafting, Kobold’s hoard reflects its scavenging. 
• Lore: Zorvathax’s corruption or Valtherion’s wilds shape these foes, mid-tier 
threats to order. 
• Challenge: Skills and stats test sub-class tactics, with Kobold traps adding 
environmental peril. 
 
Below are 5 Moderate Leveled Creatures: 
Creature Design Notes 
• Level Range: 10-15, balanced for players with sub-classes at Level 10. 
• Stats: HP, Attack, Armor, Speed (blocks/second), moderate difficulty. 

--- PAGE 268 ---
• Strengths/Weaknesses: Exploitable by job-classes like Blacksmith, Alchemist, or 
Fletcher. 
• Skills: 2 abilities with cooldowns, blending natural and magical traits. 
• Drops: Include crafting materials, Kupdun, Zirdun, and rare Skill Books. Zirdun 
drops are modest (1-5 range) to reflect moderate tier, with higher amounts tied to 
higher levels.

Weaknesses:

Combat and Crafting Synergy 
• Frostmane Wolf: Leatherworkers use Frostmane Pelt for cold-resistant gear, 
Blacksmiths craft Icefang Tooth daggers. Fire weakness aids Alchemists (Flame 
Burst potions). 
• Arcane Stag: Enchanters use Arcane Antler for runes, Leatherworkers craft Stag 
Hide boots. Physical weakness suits Warriors (Blacksmith’s Tempered Strike). 
• Venomtail Scorpion: Armorers use Scorpion Chitin for shields, Alchemists brew 
Venom Sac poisons. Light weakness favors Clerics (Runesmith’s Holy Inscription). 
• Thunderclaw Bear: Leatherworkers tan Bear Fur for armor, Blacksmiths forge 
Thunder Claw axes. Piercing weakness aids Rangers (Fletcher’s Keen Fletching 
arrows). 
• Shadowwing Owl: Fletchers craft Shadow Feather arrows, Jewelers make Owl 
Talon trinkets. Light weakness suits Clerics (Apothecary’s Restorative Touch). 
Loot Utility 
• Coins: Kupdun (75-225) and Zirdun (1-6) fund crafting (e.g., 50 Kupdun = 1 Zirdun for 
materials, 5 Zirdun for a Skill Book). 
• Materials: Support job-classes (e.g., Scorpion Chitin for Armorer, Shadow Feathers 
for Fletcher). 
• Skill Books: Enhance sub-class progression (e.g., Potent Mixture for Alchemist, 
Eagle Eye for Sniper). 
Below are 5 Moderate Leveled Human Bandits: 
Bandit Design Notes 
• Level Range: 10-15, balanced for players with sub-classes at Level 10.

• Skills: 2 abilities with cooldowns, tied to their weapons and tactics. 
• Drops: Coins (Kupdun/Zirdun), crafting materials, specific weapons (e.g., daggers, 
clubs), and rare Skill Books, scaled to level and description.

Weapon Drop Details 
• Rusty Dagger (Duskwind Cutthroat): Lightweight, stealth-focused, ideal for 
Rogues; craftable into poison-dipped blades with Alchemist skills. 
• Crude Wooden Club (Stonegale Brigand): Simple but sturdy, suited for Warriors; 
craftable into reinforced maces with Blacksmith skills. 
• Scorched Shortsword (Emberfield Arsonist): Fire-infused, versatile for 
Warriors/Rogues; craftable into flaming swords with Enchanter skills. 
• Spiked Mace (Thornskull Enforcer): Heavy-hitting, perfect for Warriors; craftable 
into enchanted maces with Runesmith skills. 
• Scavenged Crossbow (Drakemire Sniper): Ranged precision for Rangers; craftable 
into shadow-enhanced crossbows with Fletcher skills. 
 
Combat and Crafting Synergy 
• Duskwind Cutthroat: Blacksmiths sharpen Rusty Daggers, Leatherworkers craft 
Shadow Cloak Fragments. Magic weakness aids Mages (Enchanter’s Arcane 
Infusion). 
• Stonegale Brigand: Armorers use Scrap Metal with Crude Wooden Clubs, 
Enchanters craft Arcane Shards. Piercing weakness suits Rangers (Fletcher’s Keen 
Fletching). 
• Emberfield Arsonist: Leatherworkers tan Charred Leather, Alchemists brew Ember 
Powder with Scorched Shortswords. Ice weakness favors Mages (Elementalist’s Ice 
Lance). 
• Thornskull Enforcer: Blacksmiths forge Worn Plate and Spiked Maces, Tinkers use 
Dwarven Bolts. Magic weakness aids Clerics (Runesmith’s Holy Inscription). 
• Drakemire Sniper: Fletchers craft Scavenged Crossbows with Crossbow Strings, 
Jewelers use Shadow Essence. Light weakness suits Clerics (Inquisitor’s Sacred 
Flame). 
Loot Utility 
• Coins: Kupdun (75-225) and Zirdun (1-6) fund crafting (e.g., 50 Kupdun = 1 Zirdun for 
materials, 5 Zirdun for a Skill Book). 
• Materials/Weapons: Support job-classes (e.g., Spiked Mace for Blacksmith, 
Crossbow String for Fletcher). 

--- PAGE 284 ---
• Skill Books: Enhance sub-class progression (e.g., Nightcloak for Assassin, Piercing 
Bolt for Sniper). 
 
 
Below are 5 High Leveled Monsters:

Likeness Mechanic in Action 
• Trigger: If one player reaches 50 Likeness (tracked individually), the creature 
appears randomly (e.g., 20% chance per Zorvathax/Tetrad fight). 
• Aid: Fights for 5 turns with reduced stats, targeting Zorvathax or Tetrad Lieutenants 
(e.g., Soulreaver, Blightqueen). 
• Utility: Adds tactical depth (e.g., Stormcrest Gryphon dives adds, Luminant Serpent 
blinds Zorvathax), rewarding player engagement. 
Loot Utility 
• Coins: Zirdun (10-30) funds crafting (e.g., 20 Zirdun for rare materials); Guldun (0-3) 
is a premium reward. 
• Materials/Weapons: Support job-classes (e.g., Luminant Scales for Runesmith, 
Flameclaw Axe for Blacksmith). 
• Skill Books: Random rare drops enhance sub-classes (e.g., Divine Retribution for 
Paladin, Eagle Eye for Sniper). 
Below are 5 High Leveled Human Bandits: 
Bandit Design Notes 
• Level Range: 20-25, for advanced players. 
• Stats: HP, Attack, Armor, Speed (blocks/second), high difficulty. 
• Strengths/Weaknesses: Exploitable by job-classes. 
• Skills: 3 abilities with cooldowns, reflecting their bandit expertise. 
• Drops: Zirdun (10-30), Guldun (0-3), materials, weapons, and rare Skill Books. 

--- PAGE 303 ---
• Likeness Compatibility: Earned via actions (e.g., +5 per fight, +10 per aid), 
threshold at 50. Follower stats are slightly reduced (75% HP/Attack) for balance, 
with Perks tied to the player’s job-class (Warrior, Mage, Rogue, Cleric, Ranger).

b. Cooldown: 15 seconds.

Currency Drop Breakdown 
• Vyrndral Shadowblade (Level 20): 10-15 Zirdun common, 1 Guldun uncommon, 2 
Guldun rare = ~10-17 Zirdun, 0-2 Guldun total. 
• Kaltheron Forgebreaker (Level 21): 12-18 Zirdun common, 1 Guldun uncommon, 2 
Guldun rare = ~12-20 Zirdun, 0-2 Guldun total. 
• Drakemire Spellthief (Level 23): 15-20 Zirdun common, 1-2 Guldun uncommon, 3 
Guldun rare = ~15-23 Zirdun, 0-3 Guldun total. 
• Aurelith Lightreaver (Level 24): 18-25 Zirdun common, 1-2 Guldun uncommon, 3 
Guldun rare = ~18-28 Zirdun, 0-3 Guldun total. 
• Thornskull Sniper Lord (Level 25): 20-30 Zirdun common, 2 Guldun uncommon, 3 
Guldun rare = ~20-33 Zirdun, 0-3 Guldun total. 
• Scaling: Zirdun is primary (10-30), Guldun is rare (0-3), increasing with level. 
 

--- PAGE 312 ---
Weapon Drop Details 
• Shadow-Edged Dagger: Stealthy, shadow-infused for Rogues; craftable into poison 
daggers. 
• Molten Warhammer: Fiery, heavy for Warriors; craftable into blazing hammers. 
• Rune-Carved Staff: Arcane, versatile for Mages; craftable into enchanted staves. 
• Blessed Longsword: Holy, balanced for Clerics; craftable into radiant swords. 
• Rune-Etched Crossbow: Precise, ranged for Rangers; craftable into sniper 
crossbows. 
 
Combat and Crafting Synergy 
• Vyrndral Shadowblade: Leatherworkers craft Shadowthread Cloth, Alchemists use 
Poison Vials with Shadow-Edged Daggers. Light weakness aids Clerics (Holy 
Inscription). 
• Kaltheron Forgebreaker: Blacksmiths forge Forge Scrap and Molten Warhammers, 
Enchanters use Emberstone. Ice weakness suits Mages (Ice Lance). 
• Drakemire Spellthief: Enchanters craft Arcane Dust with Rune-Carved Staffs, 
Runesmiths use Spellshards. Physical weakness favors Warriors (Tempered Strike). 
• Aurelith Lightreaver: Runesmiths forge Sanctified Steel and Blessed Longswords, 
Jewelers use Light Essence. Shadow weakness aids Rogues (Nightcloak). 
• Thornskull Sniper Lord: Fletchers craft Crossbow Frames with Rune-Etched 
Crossbows, Tinkers use Trap Mechanisms. Magic weakness suits Mages (Arcane 
Infusion). 
Likeness Mechanic in Action 
• Trigger: If one player reaches 50 Likeness (tracked individually), the bandit 
becomes a Follower, joining the party permanently. 
• Follower Role: Fights alongside the player with reduced stats, using 2 of their 3 
skills, and crafts gear via Perks tailored to the player’s job-class. 
• Perk Examples:  
o Warrior player gets Forgebreaker crafting Fireforged Armor. 
o Mage player gets Spellthief crafting Rune-Infused Staffs. 
• Utility: Adds party depth (e.g., Shadowblade flanks, Sniper Lord traps), rewarding 
player interaction. 

--- PAGE 313 ---
Loot Utility 
• Coins: Zirdun (10-30) funds crafting (e.g., 20 Zirdun for rare materials); Guldun (0-3) 
is a premium reward. 
• Materials/Weapons: Support job-classes (e.g., Sanctified Steel for Runesmith, 
Rune-Etched Crossbow for Fletcher). 
• Skill Books: Random rare drops enhance sub-classes (e.g., Divine Retribution for 
Paladin, Piercing Bolt for Sniper). 
The 4 Tetrad’s: 
Tetrad Design Notes 
• Level Range: 30-35, endgame bosses for max-level players. 
• Stats: HP, Attack, Armor, Speed (blocks/second), extreme difficulty. 
• Strengths/Weaknesses: Reflect their roles, exploitable by job-classes. 
• Skills: 3-4 abilities with cooldowns, showcasing their power. 
• Drops: Guldun (5-15), Legendary Items (craftable into top-tier gear), Legendary 
Fragmented Items (sets with proportional stats), Epic Items (crafting/utility), Epic 
Weapons/Tools/Equipment (combat/crafting). 
 
1. Kaltharion, the Soulreaver 
• Level: 30 
• Region: Zorathar (Large City), Shadowfen (Small City) 
• Description: Once a revered necromancer of Valtherion’s arcane council, 
Kaltharion sought immortality through forbidden soul-binding rites. Betrayed by his 
peers, he turned to Zorvathax, who transformed him into a skeletal wraith clad in 
tattered robes, wielding a Scythe of Soulrend. Now the “Soulreaver,” he harvests 
souls to fuel Zorvathax’s war machine, his betrayal fracturing Valtherion’s magical 
defenses. 
Stats: 
• HP: 1000 
• Attack: 30-40 (shadow + necrotic) 
• Armor: 10 

--- PAGE 314 ---
• Speed: 2 blocks/second 
Strengths: 
• Resistant to shadow and necrotic damage (+75% reduction).

Weaknesses: 
• Vulnerable to light damage (+50% extra damage). 
• Moderate armor, weak to piercing attacks (ignores 50% of armor). 
Skills: 
1. Soul Harvest  
a. Effect: Deals 35-45 necrotic damage in a 5-block AoE, heals 20 HP per 
enemy hit. 
b. Cooldown: 20 seconds. 
2. Wraithbind  
a. Effect: Summons 3 Soul Wraiths (100 HP, 15-20 damage) for 15 seconds. 
b. Cooldown: 25 seconds. 
3. Scythe Sweep  
a. Effect: Deals 30-40 shadow damage in a 4-block arc, applies -5 armor for 6 
seconds. 
b. Cooldown: 15 seconds. 
Drops: 
• Minimum Coin: 5-10 Guldun 
• Legendary Item (60%):  
o Name: Soulreaver’s Mantle 
o Description: A flowing cloak woven from captured souls, pulsing with dark 
energy. 
o Craftable Into: Cloak of Eternity (+10 INT, +5 shadow resist, +20% necrotic 
damage). 
o Use: Boosts Mage/Summoner builds, enhances necrotic spells. 
• Legendary Fragmented Item (30%):  
o Name: Scythe of Soulrend Fragment (1/3 set) 
o Description: A piece of Kaltharion’s scythe, radiating soul-stealing power. 
o Full Stats: Attack 25-35, +10 necrotic damage, +5% lifesteal. 

--- PAGE 315 ---
o Fragment Stats: Attack 8-11, +3 necrotic damage (1/3rd power). 
o Use: Assemble for a top-tier necrotic weapon; fragments usable as weaker 
gear. 
• Epic Item (80%):  
o Name: Wraithbone Shard 
o Description: A jagged fragment of bone imbued with necrotic energy, 
humming with a mournful wail. 
o Quantity: 5-10. 
o Uses:  
▪ Crafting: Enchanters create Necrotic Runes (+5 necrotic damage to 
weapons); Runesmiths craft Soulbound Armor (+3 shadow resist, +10 
HP). 
▪ Utility: Consumable by Mages (Summoner) to summon a Wraith (50

• Epic Weapon (70%):  
o Name: Necrotic Scepter

▪ Combat: Boosts Mage necrotic spells (e.g., Spirit Barrage +10%

• Vulnerable to ice damage (+50% extra damage). 
• Low armor, weak to physical burst from Warriors or Rogues. 
Skills: 
1. Firestorm  
a. Effect: Deals 35-45 fire damage in a 6-block AoE, applies 10 fire 
damage/second for 6 seconds. 
b. Cooldown: 20 seconds. 
2. Flame Lash  
a. Effect: Deals 30-40 fire damage in a 5-block line, knocks back 2 blocks. 
b. Cooldown: 15 seconds. 
3. Ember Dance  
a. Effect: Gains +5 evasion and leaves a 2-block fire trail (15 damage/second) 
for 8 seconds. 
b. Cooldown: 25 seconds. 
4. Inferno Pulse  
a. Effect: Deals 20-30 fire damage in a 4-block radius, heals self for 50 HP. 
b. Cooldown: 30 seconds.

Currency Drop Summary 
• Kaltharion (Level 30): 5-10 Guldun. 
• Vyrithra (Level 32): 6-12 Guldun. 
• Gorzod (Level 33): 8-14 Guldun. 
• Serathine (Level 35): 10-15 Guldun. 
• Scaling: Guldun increases with level (5-15 range). 
 
Combat and Crafting Synergy 
• Kaltharion: Enchanters craft Wraithbone Shards/Necrotic Scepters, Runesmiths 
use Soulreaver’s Mantle fragments. Light weakness aids Clerics (Divine 
Retribution). 
• Vyrithra: Alchemists craft Corrupted Vines/Venomthorn Whips, Leatherworkers 
use Blightqueen’s Crown fragments. Fire weakness suits Mages (Ice Lance). 
• Gorzod: Blacksmiths forge Forgemaster’s Ore/Ironclad Mauls, Armorers use 
Hammer of Ruin fragments. Magic weakness favors Mages (Arcane Infusion). 
• Serathine: Enchanters craft Emberweave Thread/Pyreblade Staffs, Leatherworkers 
use Flameweaver’s Robes fragments. Ice weakness aids Warriors (Tempered Strike 
with ice). 
Lore Integration 
• Kaltharion: Weakened arcane wards, enabling Zorvathax’s invasion. 
• Vyrithra: Blighted forests, disrupting trade and food supplies. 
• Gorzod: Crushed mountain strongholds, arming Zorvathax’s forces. 
• Serathine: Ignited southern rebellions, fracturing Valtherion’s unity. 
Loot Utility 
• Guldun (5-15): Funds endgame crafting (e.g., 10 Guldun for materials). 
• Legendary Items: Craft top-tier gear (e.g., Gown of Inferno for Mages). 
• Legendary Fragmented Items: Assemble for ultimate weapons (e.g., Scythe of 
Soulrend). 
• Epic Items: Crafting/utility (e.g., Forgemaster’s Ore for Blacksmiths). 
• Epic Weapons: Combat boosts (e.g., Ironclad Maul for Warriors).

Below Is the Description and stats of the First End Game Level Boss: 
Zorvathax, the Dark Demon Lord: Shadowreaver 
• Level: 70 
• Region: Zorathar (Large City, his dark citadel) 
• Backstory: Zorvathax was once a celestial guardian of Valtherion, tasked with 
maintaining the balance between light and shadow. Eons ago, he grew envious of 
the mortal realms’ freedom, believing their chaos deserved a ruler to impose order. 
Betraying his divine kin, he plunged into the abyss, forging a pact with the primal 
darkness to become the Shadowreaver. Wielding the Crown of Umbral Dominion, 
he shattered Valtherion’s sacred seals, unleashing corruption that twisted its lands 
and people. Kaltharion’s soul-binding, Vyrithra’s blight, Gorzod’s iron conquests, 
and Serathine’s fiery betrayal were all orchestrated by Zorvathax, who promised 
them power in exchange for servitude. Now, from his obsidian throne in Zorathar, he 
seeks to enslave Valtherion entirely, his shadow stretching across the realm as a 
harbinger of eternal night. 
Stats: 
• HP: 2500 (over double Serathine’s 1100) 
• Attack: 70-90 (over double Gorzod’s 35-45) 
• Armor: 20 (double Kaltharion’s 10) 
• Speed: 4 blocks/second (exceeds Serathine’s 3) 
Strengths: 
• Resistant to shadow, necrotic, and fire damage (+75% reduction). 
• Massive HP, armor, and damage, excels at all ranges and sustained combat. 
Weaknesses: 
• Vulnerable to light and ice damage (+50% extra damage). 
• High resource demand, susceptible to coordinated burst from multiple job-classes 
(e.g., Clerics, Mages, Warriors). 
Skills: 
1. Umbral Wrath  

--- PAGE 324 ---
a. Effect: Deals 80-100 shadow damage in a 8-block AoE, applies 15 shadow 
damage/second for 8 seconds. 
b. Cooldown: 25 seconds. 
2. Crown of Dominion  
a. Effect: Summons 4 Shadow Phantoms (200 HP, 25-35 damage) for 20 
seconds and grants self +10 armor for 10 seconds. 
b. Cooldown: 30 seconds. 
3. Netherflame Surge  
a. Effect: Deals 70-90 fire damage in a 6-block cone, creates a 4-block lava 
pool (20 damage/second) for 12 seconds. 
b. Cooldown: 20 seconds. 
4. Soulreaver’s Grasp  
a. Effect: Targets one enemy, deals 60-80 necrotic damage, immobilizes for 4 
seconds, and heals Zorvathax for 100 HP. 
b. Cooldown: 25 seconds. 
 
Loot Drops 
Zorvathax’s bounty exceeds the Tetrads’, offering a minimum of 20-30 Guldun (double 
Serathine’s 10-15), alongside Legendary Materials, Legendary Weapons, and Legendary 
Skill Books. Drop chances are higher to reflect his status: Legendary Materials (80%), 
Legendary Weapons (70%), and Legendary Skill Books (60%). Each item is detailed for 
crafting, combat, or progression. 
Minimum Coin 
• Amount: 20-30 Guldun 
• Description: A hoard of gold coins stamped with Zorvathax’s shadowy sigil, 
amassed from Valtherion’s plundered treasuries. 
• Use: Funds endgame crafting (e.g., 20 Guldun for rare materials, 50 Guldun for 
Legendary upgrades).

Combat and Crafting Synergy 
• Combat:  
o Weaknesses: Light (Clerics: Divine Retribution +50% damage) and ice 
(Mages: Ice Lance +50% damage) counter his resistances. 
o Tactics: Warriors tank with Netherflame Trident, Rogues flank with 
Shadowreaver’s Fang, Mages control with Crownstaff summons. 
• Crafting:  
o Blacksmith: Netherforge Alloy into Netherforged Plate for tanks. 
o Enchanter: Umbral Essence into Shadowreaver’s Amulet for casters. 
o Runesmith: Soulshatter Crystal into Soulshatter Glyphs for lifesteal 
weapons. 
Lore Integration 
• Zorvathax’s fall corrupted Valtherion’s balance, empowering his Tetrads:  
o Kaltharion’s soul-binding breached arcane defenses. 
o Vyrithra’s blight severed trade routes. 
o Gorzod’s conquests armed his legions. 
o Serathine’s betrayal fractured southern unity. 
• His Crown of Umbral Dominion is the source of his power, a potential future quest 
item. 
Loot Utility 
• Guldun (20-30): Funds ultimate crafting (e.g., 50 Guldun for Legendary gear). 
• Materials: Enable top-tier crafting (e.g., Dominion Shield for Knights, Essence of 
Torment for Alchemists). 
• Weapons: Endgame gear (e.g., Crownstaff for Summoners, Netherflame Trident for 
Berserkers). 
• Skill Books: Unlock pinnacle skills (e.g., Soul Dominion for Cleric hybrids). 
Job-Classes in Valtherion 
1. Blacksmith: Forges weapons and armor (e.g., swords, shields). 

--- PAGE 329 ---
2. Armorer: Specializes in defensive gear (e.g., plate armor, helms). 
3. Leatherworker: Crafts leather-based gear (e.g., cloaks, boots). 
4. Fletcher: Makes ranged weapons and ammo (e.g., bows, arrows). 
5. Tinker: Constructs gadgets and traps (e.g., crossbows, explosives). 
6. Enchanter: Adds magical enhancements (e.g., runes, spell effects). 
7. Runesmith: Crafts runestones and glyphs (e.g., inscribed buffs). 
8. Alchemist: Brews potions and concoctions (e.g., healing, bombs). 
9. Jeweler: Creates accessories (e.g., rings, amulets). 
10. Apothecary: Produces healing/utility items (e.g., salves, antidotes).

Summary of Material Usage Across Job-Classes 
• Blacksmith: Forges with ores (Duskiron, Emberite), metals (Rusty Club, 
Netherforge Alloy), and claws/fangs (Shadow Claw, Ember Tooth). 
• Armorer: Crafts with hides (Prowler Skin, Goblin Skin), metals (Patched Vest, 
Forgemaster’s Ore), and ores (Frostvein, Lightstone). 
• Leatherworker: Works with pelts (Shadowclaw Skin, Tattered Cloak), cloths (Loin 
Cloth), and plants (Emberbloom, Thornskull Bramble). 
• Fletcher: Uses strings (Notched Shortbow), sticks (Heavy Stick), and plants/ores 
(Thornskull Bramble, Thornsteel). 
• Tinker: Employs scraps (Broken Iron Blade, Rusty Knife), mechanisms (Ore 
Fragments), and ores (Duskiron, Netherforge Alloy). 
• Enchanter: Enhances with shards (Wraithbone, Ember Tooth), essences (Umbral), 
and plants (Duskpetal, Frostvine). 
• Runesmith: Inscribes with crystals (Soulshatter), bones (Bone Fragments), and 
ores (Lightstone). 
• Alchemist: Brews from venoms (Venomtail Stinger, Shadow Claw), saps (Liquid 
Slime), and plants (Emberbloom, Lightroot). 
• Jeweler: Crafts with essences (Umbral), teeth (Ember Tooth), and plants/ores 
(Lightroot, Duskiron). 
• Apothecary: Heals with slime (Liquid Slime), meat (Kobold Meat), and plants 
(Frostvine, Lightroot). 
 
Integration with Valtherion 
• Low-Level Sources: Materials from Auric Slime (Liquid Slime) or Ember-Tooth 
Goblin (Ember Tooth) provide early crafting options, scaling with level. 
• Tetrad/Zorvathax: Higher-tier materials (e.g., Netherforge Alloy, Soulshatter 
Crystal) support endgame crafting. 
• Wild Resources: Plants (Duskpetal) and ores (Emberite) tie to regions, encouraging 
exploration and complementing mob drops. 
• Job-Class Depth: Every class benefits (e.g., Blacksmiths use Thornsteel Ore, 
Alchemists use Venomtail Stinger), fostering diverse playstyles.

Progression and Conquest Utility 
• Early-Game (Level 1-10): Players rely on basic crafting (e.g., Stone Hatchet, Ragtag 
Armor) and drops from low-level mobs (e.g., Emberfang Spear from Ember-Tooth 
Goblins) to survive bandits and creatures. 
• Mid-Game (Level 11-30): Access to ores (Duskiron, Emberite) and plants 
(Frostvine) enables stronger gear (e.g., Duskiron Mace, Thorned Armor), preparing 
for Tetrad encounters. 
• Late-Game (Level 31+): Drops from Tetrads and Zorvathax (e.g., Netherflame 
Trident, Gown of Inferno) provide endgame power to defeat the Dark Demon Lord, 
with items like Essence of Torment and Phantom Elixir aiding in large-scale battles. 
Durability Concept Overview 
• Durability: Represents the condition of weapons, equipment, and certain items, 
measured as a numeric value (e.g., 30/30). Each use (combat, blocking, or 
activation) reduces durability by a set amount, depending on the item’s quality and 
type. 
• Breakage: When durability reaches 0, the item becomes unusable (weapons deal 
50% damage, equipment offers no stats, items lose effects) until repaired. 
• Repair: Players and NPCs with sufficient Job-Class skill levels can restore 
durability using specific materials, with success and efficiency scaling by skill tier. 

--- PAGE 348 ---
• Skill Levels: Job-Classes (e.g., Blacksmith, Leatherworker) have 5 tiers (Novice, 
Apprentice, Journeyman, Expert, Master), each unlocking repair capabilities for 
higher-tier gear. 
 
Durability Mechanics 
1. Durability Loss:  
a. Weapons: Lose 1 durability per hit dealt (melee) or shot fired (ranged). 
Critical hits or blocked attacks cost 2 durability. 
b. Equipment: Loses 1 durability per 10 damage taken (armor) or per 5 blocks 
traveled (boots). Environmental hazards (e.g., lava, acid) double loss rates. 
c. Items: Durable items (e.g., traps, tools) lose 1 durability per use; 
consumables (e.g., potions) are single-use and lack durability. 
2. Durability Tiers:  
a. Low-Tier (Starting/Early): 15-30 durability (e.g., Wooden Club, Tattered 
Shirt). 
b. Mid-Tier: 35-50 durability (e.g., Iron Shortsword, Duskiron Helm). 
c. High-Tier (Late): 55-60+ durability (e.g., Shadowreaver’s Fang, Netherforged 
Plate). 
3. Visual Feedback:  
a. Items display durability (e.g., 25/30) in the inventory. At 25% durability, they 
glow red as a warning. Broken items (0/X) appear grayed out. 
4. Breakage Effects:  
a. Weapons: Damage reduced to 50%, no bonus effects (e.g., fire damage from 
Emberfang Spear disabled). 
b. Equipment: Stats nullified (e.g., no armor from Iron Cuirass, no speed from 
Mud Boots). 
c. Items: Durable items (e.g., Trader’s Pouch) stop functioning until repaired. 
 
Repair System 
• Who Can Repair: Players and NPCs with the appropriate Job-Class (e.g., 
Blacksmith for weapons, Leatherworker for leather gear) can repair items if their 
skill level meets or exceeds the item’s tier requirement. 
• Skill Tiers:  
o Novice (Level 1-10): Repair Low-Tier items (up to 30 

--- PAGE 349 ---
 
Boss Design Guidelines 
• Regeneration: All bosses respawn in their designated area 48 in-game hours after 
defeat. 
• Loot: Low-level bosses (3) drop high-end low-level loot with a 25% chance for Skill 
Books, Rare Weapons, Equipment, or Items. Mid-to-late-game bosses (7) drop 
progressively rarer items tied to their themes. 
• Uniqueness: Each boss has distinct mechanics, avoiding overlap with others or 
Zorvathax/Tetrads. 
 
1. Gloomspike Matriarch 
• Level: 12 (Low-Level Boss) 
• Area: Duskwind Caverns (shadowy cave network) 
• Description: A massive, spiny spider with a chitinous hide glowing faintly purple, 
commanding a brood of smaller Shadowclaw Prowlers. Her web-covered lair traps 
unwary adventurers. 
Stats: 
• HP: 60 
• Attack: 10-14 (claw swipe), 8-12 (web shot, 6-block range) 
• Armor: 3 
• Speed: 2 blocks/second 
Strengths: 
• Web Barrage: Fires sticky webs (8-12 damage, slows 50% for 5 seconds, 15-second 
cooldown). 
• Brood Summon: Spawns 2 Shadowclaw Prowlers (10 HP, 4-6 damage) every 20 
seconds. 
• Shadow Resilience: +50% shadow damage resistance. 
Weaknesses: 
• Fire Vulnerability: Takes +50% fire damage; webs burn away instantly. 

--- PAGE 350 ---
• Slow Movement: Easily kited outside her web zone.

Summary 
• Low-Level Bosses (3): Gloomspike Matriarch (Level 12), Cinderjaw Ravager (Level 
15), Rotgrasp Wraith (Level 10) match high-end low-level stats (HP 50-70, Attack 10-
15), with enhanced loot including Skill Books and rare gear (25% chance each). 
• Mid-to-Late-Level Bosses (7): Scale from Level 25 (Stormshriek Harpy) to Level 50 
(Pyreclaw Alpha, Spectral Dreadknight), offering mid-to-late-game challenges and 
loot to prepare for Zorvathax. 
• Regeneration: All respawn in their areas after 48 in-game hours, encouraging 
repeated farming. 
• Uniqueness: Each boss has distinct mechanics (e.g., web traps, fire charges, 
spectral charges), ensuring varied combat experiences. 
Food Recipe Guidelines 
• Sources: Ingredients come from merchants (e.g., Kupdun-bought goods), mob 
drops (e.g., Kobold Meat), or foraging (e.g., Duskpetal). 
• Preparation: Requires a heat source (campfire, stove) and specific Job-Class skills 
(e.g., Apothecary Level 1+). 
• Effects: Restore HP, grant buffs, or aid combat/exploration. 

--- PAGE 359 ---
• Process: Detailed steps ensure replicability in-game. 
 
Low-Level Food Recipes (Level 1-10, Early Game) 
These use basic ingredients from low-level mobs, bandits, and common foraging spots. 
1. Kobold Meat Skewer  
a. Ingredients: Kobold Meat (1, Tunnel-Skitter Kobold drop), Stick (1, foraged or 
Grime-Snout Goblin drop) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Kobold Meat), wild/foraged (Stick) 
c. Process: Skewer meat on stick, roast over campfire for 2 minutes 
(Apothecary Level 1). 
d. Effect: Restores 15 HP, +1 speed for 10 minutes. 
2. Grime Broth  
a. Ingredients: Bone Fragments (2, Shambling Husk drop), Water (1 unit, 
merchant-bought), Duskpetal (1, foraged in Shadowfen) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Bone Fragments), merchant (Water), foraged (Duskpetal) 
c. Process: Boil bones and water in a pot for 5 minutes, add Duskpetal, simmer 
2 minutes (Apothecary Level 1). 
d. Effect: Restores 10 HP, +2 shadow resist for 15 minutes. 
3. Ember-Tooth Stew  
a. Ingredients: Ember Tooth (1, Ember-Tooth Goblin drop), Water (1 unit, 
merchant), Emberbloom (1, foraged in Emberfield) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Ember Tooth), merchant (Water), foraged (Emberbloom) 
c. Process: Simmer tooth and water for 4 minutes, stir in Emberbloom, cook 3 
minutes (Apothecary Level 2). 
d. Effect: Restores 20 HP, +3 fire resist for 20 minutes. 
4. Shadowclaw Soup  
a. Ingredients: Shadow Claw (1, Shadowclaw Prowler drop), Water (1 unit, 
merchant), Thornskull Bramble (1, foraged in Thornskull Hold) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Shadow Claw), merchant (Water), foraged (Thornskull 
Bramble) 
c. Process: Boil claw in water for 5 minutes, add bramble, cook 2 minutes 
(Apothecary Level 1). 
d. Effect: Restores 15 HP, +2 poison resist for 15 minutes. 
5. Rat Tail Porridge  

--- PAGE 360 ---
a. Ingredients: Venomtail Stinger (1, Venomtail Rat drop), Grain (1 unit, 
merchant), Water (1 unit, merchant) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Venomtail Stinger), merchant (Grain, Water) 
c. Process: Boil grain and water for 3 minutes, mash in stinger, simmer 2 
minutes (Apothecary Level 1). 
d. Effect: Restores 12 HP, cures minor poison. 
6. Ragtag Flatbread  
a. Ingredients: Flour (1 unit, merchant), Patched Vest Scraps (1, Ragtag Thug 
drop), Water (1 unit, merchant) 
b. Source: Merchant (Flour, Water), mob drop (Patched Vest Scraps) 
c. Process: Mix flour and water into dough, knead in vest scraps, bake on 
campfire for 3 minutes (Apothecary Level 2). 
d. Effect: Restores 10 HP, +1 armor for 10 minutes. 
7. Slinkfoot Stew  
a. Ingredients: Tattered Cloak (1, Slinkfoot Cutthroat drop), Water (1 unit, 
merchant), Duskpetal (1, foraged in Shadowfen) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Tattered Cloak), merchant (Water), foraged (Duskpetal) 
c. Process: Boil cloak and water for 5 minutes, add Duskpetal, simmer 2 
minutes (Apothecary Level 2). 
d. Effect: Restores 15 HP, +2 DEX for 15 minutes. 
8. Grubstake Biscuit  
a. Ingredients: Flour (1 unit, merchant), Poorly Fletched Arrow (1, Grubstake 
Archer drop), Water (1 unit, merchant) 
b. Source: Merchant (Flour, Water), mob drop (Poorly Fletched Arrow) 
c. Process: Grind arrow into powder, mix with flour and water, bake on 
campfire for 4 minutes (Apothecary Level 1). 
d. Effect: Restores 10 HP, +1 range attack for 10 minutes. 
9. Mudboot Mash  
a. Ingredients: Mud-Caked Boots (1, Mudboot Brawler drop), Grain (1 unit, 
merchant), Water (1 unit, merchant) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Mud-Caked Boots), merchant (Grain, Water) 
c. Process: Scrape mud from boots, boil with grain and water for 5 minutes 
(Apothecary Level 2). 
d. Effect: Restores 12 HP, +1 swamp speed for 20 minutes. 
10. Torchlight Roast  
a. Ingredients: Charred Leathers (1, Torchlight Brigand drop), Emberbloom (1, 
foraged in Emberfield), Salt (1 unit, merchant) 

--- PAGE 361 ---
b. Source: Mob drop (Charred Leathers), foraged (Emberbloom), merchant 
(Salt) 
c. Process: Season leathers with salt, roast with Emberbloom over campfire 
for 4 minutes (Apothecary Level 2). 
d. Effect: Restores 15 HP, +2 fire resist for 15 minutes. 
 
Mid-Level Food Recipes (Level 11-30, Mid-Game) 
These incorporate mid-tier mob drops, merchant goods, and rarer foraged items. 
11. Duskiron Broth  
a. Ingredients: Duskiron Ore (1, foraged in Shadowfen), Water (2 units, 
merchant), Kobold Meat (1, Tunnel-Skitter Kobold drop) 
b. Source: Foraged (Duskiron Ore), merchant (Water), mob drop (Kobold Meat) 
c. Process: Melt ore into broth base, add water and meat, simmer 6 minutes 
(Alchemist Level 3). 
d. Effect: Restores 25 HP, +3 shadow resist for 30 minutes. 
12. Emberite Cakes  
a. Ingredients: Emberite Ore (1, foraged in Kaltheron), Flour (1 unit, merchant), 
Emberbloom (1, foraged in Emberfield) 
b. Source: Foraged (Emberite Ore, Emberbloom), merchant (Flour) 
c. Process: Grind ore into powder, mix with flour, add Emberbloom, bake for 5 
minutes (Apothecary Level 3). 
d. Effect: Restores 20 HP, +3 fire damage for 20 minutes. 
13. Frostvine Jelly  
a. Ingredients: Frostvine (2, foraged in Frostmead), Sugar (1 unit, merchant), 
Water (1 unit, merchant) 
b. Source: Foraged (Frostvine), merchant (Sugar, Water) 
c. Process: Boil Frostvine and water for 4 minutes, add sugar, cool into jelly 
(Apothecary Level 3). 
d. Effect: Restores 20 HP, +3 ice resist for 30 minutes. 
14. Lightroot Bread  
a. Ingredients: Lightroot (1, foraged in Aurelith), Flour (1 unit, merchant), Water 
(1 unit, merchant) 
b. Source: Foraged (Lightroot), merchant (Flour, Water) 
c. Process: Grind Lightroot into flour mix, knead with water, bake for 5 minutes 
(Apothecary Level 3). 

--- PAGE 362 ---
d. Effect: Restores 25 HP, +3 light resist for 20 minutes. 
15. Thornsteel Pasty

b. Source: Foraged (Thornsteel Ore), merchant (Grain), mob drop (Shadow 
Claw) 
c. Process: Grind ore into crust, fill with claw and grain, bake for 6 minutes 
(Apothecary Level 4). 
d. Effect: Restores 30 HP, +2 armor for 30 minutes. 
16. Auric Slime Gel

Frostvine (1, foraged in Frostmead) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Liquid Slime), merchant (Sugar), foraged (Frostvine)

d. Effect: Restores 20 HP, +2 armor for 20 minutes. 
17. Goblin Skin Crisp  
a. Ingredients: Goblin Skin (1, Ember-Tooth Goblin drop), Salt (1 unit, 
merchant), Emberbloom (1, foraged in Emberfield) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Goblin Skin), merchant (Salt), foraged (Emberbloom)

d. Effect: Restores 15 HP, +2 fire resist for 15 minutes. 
18. Prowler Stew

b. Source: Mob drop (Prowler Skin), merchant (Water), foraged (Duskpetal)

d. Effect: Restores 25 HP, +1 HP regen/minute for 30 minutes. 
19. Trader’s Pie  
a. Ingredients: Patchwork Coat (1, Scrounge-Trader drop), Flour (1 unit,

Additional Mid-to-Late-Game Recipes (Mixed Tiers) 
These expand variety with unique combinations. 
31. Stormshriek Skewers  
a. Ingredients: Storm Feathers (3, Stormshriek Harpy drop), Stick (1, foraged), 
Salt (1 unit, merchant) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Storm Feathers), foraged (Stick), merchant (Salt) 
c. Process: Skewer feathers, season with salt, roast for 5 minutes (Apothecary 
Level 4). 
d. Effect: Restores 35 HP, +3 evasion for 30 minutes. 
32. Ironveil Broth  
a. Ingredients: Thornsteel Ore (2, Ironveil Sentinel drop), Water (2 units, 
merchant), Lightroot (1, foraged in Aurelith) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Thornsteel Ore), merchant (Water), foraged (Lightroot) 
c. Process: Melt ore into broth, add water and Lightroot, simmer 6 minutes 
(Alchemist Level 4). 
d. Effect: Restores 40 HP, +5 armor for 30 minutes. 
33. Blightroot Soup  
a. Ingredients: Corrupted Vine (3, Blightroot Titan drop), Water (2 units, 
merchant), Frostvine (1, foraged in Frostmead) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Corrupted Vine), merchant (Water), foraged (Frostvine) 

--- PAGE 366 ---
c. Process: Boil vines and water for 7 minutes, add Frostvine, simmer 3 
minutes (Alchemist Level 5). 
d. Effect: Restores 45 HP, +5 poison resist for 1 hour. 
34. Frostfang Stew  
a. Ingredients: Frostspire Fur (3, Frostfang Behemoth drop), Water (2 units, 
merchant), Frostvine (2, foraged in Frostmead) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Frostspire Fur), merchant (Water), foraged (Frostvine) 
c. Process: Boil fur and water for 6 minutes, add Frostvine, cook 4 minutes 
(Apothecary Level 5). 
d. Effect: Restores 50 HP, +5 ice resist for 1 hour. 
35. Luminant Broth  
a. Ingredients: Luminant Scales (3, Luminant Overlord drop), Water (2 units, 
merchant), Lightroot (2, foraged in Aurelith) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Luminant Scales), merchant (Water), foraged (Lightroot) 
c. Process: Simmer scales in water for 8 minutes, add Lightroot, cook 4 
minutes (Alchemist Level 5). 
d. Effect: Restores 55 HP, +5 light damage for 1 hour. 
36. Pyreclaw Roast  
a. Ingredients: Ironscale Plate (3, Pyreclaw Alpha drop), Salt (1 unit, 
merchant), Emberite Ore (1, foraged in Kaltheron) 
b. Source: Mob drop (Ironscale Plate), merchant (Salt), foraged (Emberite Ore) 
c. Process: Season plate with salt, roast with ore for 6 minutes (Apothecary 
Level 5). 
d. Effect: Restores 50 HP, +5 fire resist for 1 hour.

Integration with Valtherion 
• Sourcing: Ingredients span all game tiers—low-level mobs (e.g., Kobold Meat), mid-
tier foraging (e.g., Frostvine), and high-tier drops (e.g., Umbral Essence)—ensuring 
accessibility and progression. 
• Job-Class Tie-In: Recipes require Apothecary (healing-focused) or Alchemist 
(effect-focused) skills, scaling from Level 1 (Novice) to Level 5 (Master), aligning 
with the repair system. 
• Utility: Foods restore HP (10-60) and provide buffs (e.g., +5 fire damage, +3 armor), 
critical for surviving bosses like Pyreclaw Alpha or Zorvathax. 
• Crafting Process: Each recipe uses a campfire or stove, with precise steps (e.g., 
boil, roast, bake), making it immersive and replicable. 
Merchant Design Guidelines 
• Location: Tied to specific regions in Valtherion, influencing their stock. 
• Habits: Quirks or behaviors that define their personality and interactions. 
• Specialization: Focus on specific item categories (e.g., weapons, food ingredients, 
rare materials). 
• Likeness Compatibility: A 0-100 scale (0 = hostile, 100 = ally) affecting prices and 
stock. Starts at 25 (neutral) unless specified. 

--- PAGE 370 ---
• Building Likeness: Scenarios, missions, or actions increase compatibility, detailed 
below.

Likeness Compatibility System 
• Scale: 0-100 (0 = refuses trade, 25 = neutral, 50 = friendly, 75 = trusted, 100 = ally). 
• Benefits:  
o 0-25: Full price, basic stock. 
o 26-50: 10% discount, slightly better stock. 
o 51-75: 20% discount, rare items unlock. 
o 76-100: 25-30% discount, exclusive items/missions. 
• Starting Value: Varies by merchant (15-35), reflecting personality or region. 
Options to Build Likeness Compatibility 
1. Gift-Giving: Offer items they like (e.g., Emberbloom for Emberly Scorchdeal, +10 
Likeness).  
a. Scenario: Bring 5 Emberbloom to Emberly; she crafts you a free Emberflare 
Shard (+15 total). 
2. Completing Missions: Unique tasks tied to their needs.  
a. Scenario: Clear Gloomspike Matriarch for Varn Gloomstock (+20 Likeness, 
unlocks Gloomfang Dagger). 
3. Trading Volume: Spend 100+ Kupdun with them (+5 per 100 Kupdun spent).  
a. Scenario: Buy 10 Duskpetal from Murkwell Tink (+5 Likeness). 
4. Region Assistance: Help their area (e.g., kill 10 Venomtail Rats near Bjorn 
Icebarter, +15 Likeness).  
a. Scenario: Deliver 5 Frostvine to Olaf Chillstock from a rival trader (+10 
Likeness). 
5. Personal Favors: Unique interactions based on habits.  
a. Scenario: Tell Griselda Rotfoot a ghost story (+10 Likeness, +5 more if it’s 
good). 
6. Crafting for Them: Make items they request using their materials.  
a. Scenario: Craft Torchlight Roast for Torvald Flamehand with his Charred 
Leathers (+15 Likeness). 
7. Combat Aid: Defend them from mobs or bandits.  
a. Scenario: Protect Gritmuk Scrounge from a Ragtag Thug ambush (+20 
Likeness). 

--- PAGE 378 ---
8. Story Engagement: Answer their questions or riddles correctly.  
a. Scenario: Solve Gideon Wraithdeal’s riddle about Wraithmoor (+15 
Likeness). 
9. Rare Item Trade: Sell them high-tier drops (e.g., Umbral Essence to Zargoth 
Nethertrade, +25 Likeness).  
a. Scenario: Trade a Soulshatter Crystal to Zargoth (+25 Likeness, unlocks 
Netherflame Trident purchase). 
10. Repeat Business: Visit weekly for small trades (+2 Likeness per visit, max +10).  
a. Scenario: Buy Salt from Bork Grubstake 5 times (+10 Likeness total). 
 
Integration with Valtherion 
• Regional Tie-In: Merchants reflect their areas (e.g., Frostmead’s Bjorn sells 
Frostvine goods, Kaltheron’s Pyroxis offers Ironscale Plate), enhancing immersion. 
• Inventory Synergy: Stock includes crafting materials (e.g., Water, Flour), mob 
drops (e.g., Shadow Claw), and crafted foods (e.g., Wraithbone Soup), supporting 
the 50 recipes. 
• Likeness Progression: Building rapport unlocks rare gear (e.g., Dreadblade, 
Pyreclaw Lance), aiding the fight against Zorvathax. 
• Variety: 30 merchants cover all job-classes (Blacksmith, Alchemist, etc.) and 
playstyles, from weapons to potions. 
Valtherion Map Cities 
1. Murkfen Hamlet (Shadowfen Bog) - Swampy, cursed settlement. 
2. Ashforge Village (Emberfield Ashlands) - Volcanic crafting hub. 
3. Frosthaven (Frostmead Tundra) - Icy survival outpost. 
4. Aurelith Sanctuary (Aurelith Plains) - Holy refuge. 
5. Thornskull Outpost (Thornskull Hold) - Bandit stronghold. 
6. Stormwatch Spire (Stormcrest Peaks) - Windy cliff town. 
7. Grimholt Keep (Grimholt Bastion) - Ruined fortress settlement. 
8. Eldergrove Hollow (Eldergrove Depths) - Corrupted forest village. 
9. Kaltheron Forge (Kaltheron Caldera) - Volcanic industrial center. 
10. Wraithmoor Rest (Wraithmoor Crypts) - Haunted burial town.
